# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_31413938.pdf
1 
 
SPONSORSHIP AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
HOLLYFRONTIER CHEYENNE RENEWABLE DIESEL COMPANY, LLC 
 
1. 
Parties. The parties to this Agreement are the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a municipality 
organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 
82001 (“City”), and HollyFrontier Cheyenne Renewable Diesel Company, LLC, a limited liability 
company duly organized under the laws of the State of Delaware, 300 Morrie Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming 82007 (“HollyFrontier”). 
 
2. 
Purpose of the Agreement.  The purpose of this Agreement is to designate HollyFrontier 
as the Presenting Sponsor of the 2022 Superday, the 2022 Superday Tour de Prairie, and the 2022 
Superday 5K Walk/Run, a Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events Summer Celebration 
which is annually hosted in June to kick off Recreation month in July.  This Agreement designates 
HollyFrontier as the Presenting Sponsor with no liability implied, expressed, or written at any 
event. 
 
3. 
Term.  The term of this Agreement will be from the date of the last signature affixed until 
June 30, 2022. 
 
4. 
Payment.  The City of Cheyenne will provide HollyFrontier an invoice to process 
payment directly to the vendor.  HollyFrontier agrees to pay the City the amount of Twenty-Five 
Thousand Dollars ($25,000.00) pursuant to the terms of this Agreement. 
 
5. 
City’s Responsibilities.  
 
 
A. 
As the Presenting Sponsor of the 2022 Superday, the 2022 Superday Tour de 
Prairie, and the 2022 Superday Family Fun 5K Walk/Run, in exchange for Twenty-Five Thousand 
Dollars ($25,000.00) of sponsorship revenue to the City, HollyFrontier will receive: 
 
i. 
Brand recognition as the presenting sponsor on all print (posters, brochures, 
banners, registration forms, and newspaper advertising), radio, social media (Facebook, 
www.CheyenneEvents.org, www.CheyenneRec.org), and television recognition as the 
Presenting Sponsor of the 2022 Superday, the 2022 Superday Tour de Prairie, and the 2022  
Superday Family 5K Walk/Run; 
 
ii. 
Two main entry locations with tents, tables, chairs, and banner recognizing 
HollyFrontier as the Presenting Sponsor; 
 
iii. 
Superday brochures for HollyFrontier employees to distribute to and engage 
with participants; 
 
By Stefanie Boster at 1:28 pm, Feb 23, 2022

2 
 
iv. 
Ten complimentary registrations for the Superday Tour de Prairie; and 
 
v. 
Twenty complimentary registrations for the Superday Family 5K 
Walk/Run. 
 
6. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
A. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to this 
Agreement, which are mutually agreed upon by the parties, will be incorporated by writing. 
 
 
B. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
Agreement is governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The courts of the State of Wyoming 
shall have jurisdiction over this Agreement and parties, and the venue shall be in the First Judicial 
District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
 
C. 
Compliance with Laws.  HollyFrontier agrees to keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
Agreement. 
 
 
D. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement shall 
be in writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
 
 
E. 
Termination.  Either party may termination this Agreement for cause without 
liability in the event either party fails to perform. 
 
 
F. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and 
employees, does not waive its governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to 
the extent necessary for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written 
terms of the Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retain all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., 
and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement 
actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly construed, either against 
or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental or sovereign immunity shall be 
construed in favor of governmental immunity. 
 
 
G. 
Indemnification.  Each party to this Agreement shall assume the risk of any 
liability arising from its own conduct.  Neither party agrees to insure, defend, or indemnify the 
other. 
 
 
 

3 
 
 
H. 
Reporting.  The City of Cheyenne will provide HollyFrontier an estimated media 
value (paid and in-kind) and list of elements the vendor is spending/using to promote the event. 
 
 
I. 
Entirety of Agreement.  This Agreement represents the entire and integrated 
Agreement between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral.  
 
 
In witness thereof, the parties to this Agreement, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this Agreement on the days and dates set out below and certify that 
they have read, understood and agreed to the terms and conditions of this Agreement.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
HollyFrontier Cheyenne Renewable Diesel Company, LLC

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_80080131.pdf
1 
 
Modification #1 to Contract #7405 
 
CONTINGENCY AND DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT 
 
Microsoft Datacenter:  2021 Expansion 
 
 
This Agreement is entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal 
corporation organized under the laws of the State of Wyoming (“City”), 2101 O’Neil Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Microsoft (Company), a Washington corporation (“Company”), 
This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
WHEREAS, the purpose of the Business Ready Community Program (“BRC”) is to 
promote economic development at the city, town, and county levels in order to create additional 
economic health and a stronger state economy; and 
 
WHEREAS, the BRC program allows for grants to defray the costs of communication and 
electrical utilities to encourage the development, recruitment or expansion of data generation, and 
information technology storage operations; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City is an eligible Applicant to the BRC; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City will comply and adhere to the Wyoming Preference Act (Wyo. Stat. 
§§ 16-6-201 through 16-6-206) and all other Wyoming Statutes that apply; and 
WHEREAS, the Company will contribute economically to the community by creating jobs, 
investing in development of facilities, and broadening the City’s economic and tax bases. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the promises and agreements contained herein, 
the parties agree as follows: 
 
Article I. 
Project Description 
 
 
The City shall apply for and obtain for the ultimate benefit of the Company, a BRC grant 
for the purpose of reducing operating costs at the Company’s data center facility.  It is anticipated 
by the parties that these reduced costs will provide public benefits by allowing the Company to 
generate revenues, increase employment, make investments, and otherwise expand the beneficial 
economic impact of its operations in the City of Cheyenne and the State of Wyoming. 
 
Article II. 
City’s Responsibilities 
 
 
 The City shall: 
 
A. 
Prepare and submit a BRC grant application for a Grant (“Grant”) in the amount of 
Two Million Two Hundred Fifty Thousand Dollars ($2,250,000.00). 
 
B. 
Accept the Grant award upon approval by the Wyoming State Loan and Investment 
board and undertake all project administration requirements of the Grant. 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:39 am, Feb 25, 2022

2 
 
 
C. 
Contingent upon award of the Grant and upon receipt of invoices substantiating 
communication and electrical utility expenses incurred by the Company including, 
without limitation, any such costs incurred in the commissioning of its facility, and 
upon receipt by the City or its agent from the Company of evidence towards 
meeting public benefit requirements set forth below, the City shall seek 
reimbursement of 100% of these costs from the Grant as provided under guidance 
from the Wyoming Business Council.  The Company shall provide City with 
detailed invoices, which shall be submitted to the City’s Grants Manager.  Payment 
shall be made in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 16-6-602 (as amended). 
 
Article III. 
Company’s Responsibilities  
 
The Company shall, contingent upon (i) the award of the Grant on terms acceptable to the 
Company by not later than June 30, 2022, and (ii) the Company’s obtaining all necessary permits 
(collectively, the “Conditions”): 
 
A. 
Provide in consideration for the Grant, the following public benefits: 
1.  
Expand its existing data center operations in the City of Cheyenne by 
investing approximately Fifty Million Dollars ($50,000,000.00) in on-site 
site development and/or construction. 
2.  
Pay or invest no less than Fifty-One Million Four Hundred Six Thousand 
Two Hundred Fifty Dollars ($51,406,250.00) of combined wages and 
capital expenditures within five (5) years of first receiving Grant funds. No 
less than One Million Four Hundred Six Thousand and Two Hundred Fifty 
Dollars ($1,406,250.00) of this amount shall be wages. The wages counted 
towards these amounts must be paid to positions with wage rates that exceed 
$24.14 per hour (150% of most recently announced average weekly wage 
for Laramie County per Wyoming Department of Employment as of July 1, 
2021). 
 
3.  
Microsoft will make good-faith efforts to work with the City, the political 
subdivision, and Laramie County on community initiatives once the data 
center is operational.   
 
 
B. 
Company will provide to the City an annual certification of payroll and investment 
as evidence towards meeting the public benefit requirements set forth above for a 
period of five (5) years. Furthermore, the Company understands that additional 
documentation or evidence of payrolls and investment or other public benefit may 
be required by the Wyoming Business Council and the Company agrees to provide 
such additional documentation or evidence as may be reasonably required to 
substantiate the amount of wages and capital expenditures paid or invested by the 
Company. 
 
 
C. 
Promptly repay with all Grant funds paid to it during the elapsed term of the Grant 
by the City plus interest at 2% per annum, in the event that it ceases operating its 

3 
 
data center facility in the City of Cheyenne within five (5) years of first receiving 
Grant funds from the City.  Temporary cessations of operations arising out of 
casualty 
damage, 
or 
repairs, 
maintenance, 
server 
migrations, 
or 
renovations/upgrades shall not be considered ceasing operations for purposes of 
this Subsection C.  Repayment of such Grant funds including the rate of return shall 
be the exclusive remedy of the City for any failure by the Company to perform its 
obligations under this Article III. 
 
 
D. 
Provide information, assistance, and consultation to the City in the preparation and 
submission of the BRC application. 
 
E. 
Maintain any and all records, documents, and data pertaining to job creation, wages 
paid, capital investment, and other consideration provided in exchange for the Grant 
funds and, subject to the Company’s reasonable confidentiality requirements, 
provide these records to the City upon request by the City.  Company’s records are 
deemed confidential business information and trade secret and will not be subject 
to Freedom of Information Act requests. 
F. 
Comply with all other requirements of the Grant, to the extent approved by the 
Company at the time of Grant award. 
 
Article IV. 
Parties Common Agreements 
 
A. 
The parties acknowledge and agree that the period of payments from the Grant is 
three (3) years from the date of the Grant agreement between the Wyoming 
Business Council and the City.  Both parties acknowledge and agree that the 
performance period of the Company to provide public benefits is five (5) years from 
the date of the first payment of Grant funds to the Company.  
 
B. 
The parties agree that this Agreement is contingent upon receipt of Grant funds by 
the City and is intended to meet the first level of convincing evidence of 
development and public benefit as required by the BRC program. 
 
C. 
The parties agree that in the event that the City does not receive an award of the 
Grant from the Wyoming Business Council by June 30, 2022, that no party has any 
obligation under this Agreement and that this Agreement is void unless extended 
in writing by the parties. 
Article V. 
Breach and Termination 
Subject to the provisions of Article III, Subsection C, in the event that any party breaches 
this Agreement the other parties shall be entitled to an injunction or other appropriate remedy in 
order to compel the breaching party to abide by its obligations set forth herein. 
 
 
 
 

4 
 
Article VI. 
Miscellaneous 
A. 
This Agreement consisting of six (6) pages represents the entire and integrated 
agreement and understanding between the parties and supersedes all prior 
negotiations, statements, representations, and agreements, whether written or oral. 
B. 
Neither this Agreement, nor any rights or obligations hereunder, shall be assigned 
or delegated by a party without the prior written consent of the other party. 
C. 
This Agreement shall be modified only by a written agreement, duly executed by 
all parties hereto and with the approval of the Wyoming Business Council’s Chief 
Executive Officer. 
D. 
The parties understand and agree this Agreement shall be governed by and 
interpreted pursuant to the laws of the State of Wyoming without regard to its 
conflicts of laws principles. If any dispute arises between the parties from or 
concerning this Agreement or the subject matter hereof, any suit or proceeding at 
law or in equity shall be brought in the District Court of the State of Wyoming, 
First Judicial District, sitting at Cheyenne, Wyoming. The foregoing provisions of 
this paragraph are agreed by the parties to be a material inducement to the Company 
and the City in executing this Agreement.  
E. 
The City does not waive its Governmental/Sovereign Immunity, as provided by any 
applicable law including Wyo. Stat. §§ 1-39-101, et seq., (hereinafter referred to as 
the “Act”) by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for the 
parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of this 
Agreement.  Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar 
provisions should not be construed as a waiver of sovereign immunity.  Any actions 
or claims against the City under this Agreement, to the extent that the same are 
permissible under the terms of this Section and/or applicable law, must be brought 
in accordance with the procedural requirements of the Act.  Further, the City fully 
retains all immunities and defenses provided by law with regard to any action, 
whether in tort, contract or any other theory of law, based on this Agreement. 
F. 
Nothing in this Agreement is intended by the parties or shall be construed to create 
an employer/employee or principal/agent relationship between the parties.  The 
parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity the status of third-
party beneficiary, and this Agreement is not to be construed so as to create such 
status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate 
only between the parties to this Agreement and shall inure solely to the benefit of 
the parties to this Agreement. 
G. 
The Company shall release, indemnify, and hold harmless the City and its officers, 
agents, employees, successors and assigns from any cause of action, or claims or 
demands arising out of the Company’s performance of its obligations under to the 
City under Article III, Sections A.2 and A.3, and Article III, Sections B through E, 
of this Agreement. 

5 
 
H. 
The Company shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., and the Americans with 
Disabilities Act (ADA), 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq. The Company shall assure that 
no person is discriminated against based on the grounds of age, sex, race, religion, 
national origin, or disability in connection with the performance of this Agreement.  
I. 
The parties agree to comply with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and 
regulations in the performance of this Agreement.  
J. 
All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this Agreement shall be in 
writing and given to the parties either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
K. 
The term of this Agreement is from the date of the last signature affixed below 
through five (5) years from the receipt by the Company of the first payment of 
Grant funds provided under this Agreement. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
[REMAINDER OF PAGE INTENTIONALLY LEFT BLANK] 
 
 

6

CONTRACT MODIFICATION 
Contract Modification:  
# 1
Project:  
Carey Avenue Multi Use Path
Project Number: 
Contract #7430
Contractor:  
Reiman Corp.   
Date Prepared: 
January 24, 2022 
The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these 
amendments and modifications of the plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All 
other aspects of the original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect. 
 
I. Description of Changes
This contract modification is to provide additional funding for unforeseen complications with the 
existing irrigation system. 
II. New Contract Items
There is no change in the scope of work however, a quote has been included for the required 
changes that reflect negotiated, reduced pricing due to an increase in item quantities 
($19,484.00). We anticipate similar complications as this project progresses and therefore are 
requesting an additional $10,000.00 over the quoted amount for a total of $29,484.00. These 
funds will be added to the balance of the existing Force Account. Itemized billing against this 
line item will be required for each pay request as necessary. 
 
III. Schedule
There will be no impact to the completion date of this contract.
IV. Summary

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_49426582.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
March 9, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-14-22 for furnishing Two (2) New 2022 4WD 72” Front-
Mount Mowers, for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events 
Department- Parks Division 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #E-14-22 was solicited for furnishing Two (2) New 2022 4WD 72” Front-Mount Mowers, for 
the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation & Events Department- Parks Division.   
 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Bids were received from: 
1. LL Johnson Distributing Company.  Denver, CO. 
 
2. Heartland Kubota.  Sheridan, WY. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Sam Payne (Parks), and Jason Sanchez (CRE).  Their recommendation 
is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Heartland Kubota, for the purchase of Two (2) New 
2022 4WD 72” Front-Mount Mowers.  The total cost, less Trade Ins, is $74,350.00. 
 
Additional Background Information:   
The Governing Body approved Bid E-1-22 on August 23, 2021, to Heartland Kubota for the 
purchase of Two (2) New 2022 4WD 72” Front-Mount Mowers, for the City of Cheyenne, 
Community Recreation & Events Department- Parks Division.  The Kubota Tractor Corporation 
ceased production of the awarded model due to problems with the operation of the DPF (Diesel 
Particulate Filter) system on the F3990 series mowers.  As Heartland Kubota was unable to fulfill 
this order due to unavoidable problems with manufacturing, the City cancelled the awarded of Bid 
E-1-22, and resolicited under this Bid E-14-22.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
March 7, 2022 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and MED-ENG, LLC, 
for the purchase of two (2) Bomb Suits for the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police 
Department 
______________________________________________ 
This is a single source purchase through MED-ENG, LLC, therefore no bids were requested.  The 
MED-ENG, LLC Sole Manufacturer Statement is included for reference. 
The City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department, was awarded a grant for the purchase of two 
(2) Bomb Suits, from a U.S. Department of Homeland Security Grant.   
The bomb suit requested by the Cheyenne Police Department is a Sole Source product, 
manufactured and sold exclusively through MED-ENG, LLC. 
The safety and functionality of the bomb suit is critical to the safety of the end user, and there are 
currently only two (2) bomb suits on the open market that are compliant with the NIJ 0117.01 
Public Safety Bomb Suit Standard, Safety Certification (Certified Products List from Safety 
Equipment Institute, An Affiliate Of ASTM International) is included for reference. 
The Cheyenne Police Department conducted research of both National Institute of Justice (NIJ) 
0117.01 compliant bomb suits.  Sergeant Zack Johnson (Cheyenne PD), along with Cheyenne 
Police Department Bomb Squad, conducted a review of the product offering from NP Aerospace, 
and concluded that there are several functions on the bomb suit that are not comparable to what 
MED-ENG, LLC is able to offer. For example, the bomb suit visor on the NP Aerospace helmet 
is very small, and does not appear to offer near the field of view that the MED-ENG, LLC EOD 
10E Helmet offers.  User visibility is critical while the suit is in use. 
As this procurement is conducted via a Federal Grant, MED-ENG, LLC’s active SAM.gov 
registration is included for reference.  Additionally, pricing research was conducted via GSA 
Advantage and buying the product directly from MED-ENG, LLC is less expensive, than if 
purchased via a GSA Cooperative Contract. 
 

The MED-ENG, LLC product has been recommended for approval by the City of Cheyenne Police 
Department, and is the basis for this request for Sole Source purchase.  The total requested amount 
is for $71,318.00 for the purchase of two (2) bomb suits, in accordance with the included vendor 
price quotation. 
 
Funding: 
025-21-2111-41-46528 (Federal Homeland Security Grant), Project #220020 
$71,318.00

STATE REASON FOR SOLE OR SINGLE SOURCE PURCHASE (CIRCLE ONE): 
After a thorough review of available bomb suits, Bomb Technicians on the team all agreed that Med-Eng EOD 10 
suits are the only one that meet our requirements and capabilities. Specifically, it is the only suit that meets standards for; 
fragmentation foot protection, helmet audio and hearing protection, drag rescue system, and 
electromagnetic interference and electromagnetic compatibility.

Re: Sole Manufacturer Statement – Med-Eng Protective Equipment 
 
This letter confirms that Med-Eng Holdings ULC, and Med-Eng, LLC (“Med-Eng”), is the sole 
manufacturer of Med-Eng branded personal protective equipment (PPE) for Explosive 
Ordnance Disposal (EOD) and blast related operations. This equipment includes but is not 
limited to the EOD 10E Bomb Suit and Helmet ensemble.  
 
All manufacturing is conducted at our production facilities in Ogdensburg, New York, USA and 
in Pembroke, Ontario, Canada, or at a sub-contracted facility solely on behalf of Med-Eng.  
 
The EOD 10E is distinguished by the following set of specifications for the protection, safety, 
operability, and stress mitigation of the EOD operator. 
 
NIJ 0117.01 Certification - The EOD 10E is “Certified” to the NIJ 0117.01 Public Safety Bomb 
Suit Standard. Proof of certification includes being listed on the public website of the Safety 
Equipment Institute (SEI) certification body www.seinet.org. 
Note: SEI is the only organization that can confirm certification. Self-certification is not possible. 
A manufacturer or vendor claiming their EOD suit and or helmet “is compliant”, “meets”, 
“passes” or “is tested to” the NIJ 0117.01 Standard in the absence of official certification are not 
acceptable substitutes for proof of certification. 
Additional criteria that exceed the minimum requirements of the NIJ 0117.01 Standard are listed 
below and will serve to enhance the EOD technician’s blast protection, mission safety, fitment 
and operational efficiency. 
Electromagnetic Interference and Electromagnetic Compatibility – The EOD 10E Suit and 
Helmet ensemble, in its base configuration, complies with the MIL-STD-461F and the Def-Stan 
59-411 Class A Standards as listed in the following table, to limit both radiated electromagnetic 
emissions and susceptibility to electromagnetic radiation, as proven by documentation from an 
accredited NVLAP test facility.  
EMC

Helmet Audio and Hearing Protection – The EOD 10E Helmet includes stereophonic 
(left/right) integrated speakers built into the Helmet, with individually adjustable audio levels. 
The EOD 10E Helmet includes integrated protection against blast overpressure and audio noise 
in excess of 90 dBA. The noise level cut off threshold is adjustable by the user. 
Enhanced Fragmentation Face Shield Protection – The EOD 10E Helmet face shield is 
compatible with an optional steel visor to enhance V50 fragmentation protection at the face 
level, beyond the minimum NIJ 0117.01 V50 requirements. 
Fragmentation Foot Protection – The EOD 10E Suit includes 360-degree fragmentation 
protection to the foot and fits on American foot sizes of 6 to 14. The V50 level fragmentation 
protection is at least 450 m/s (1,475 feet/second) against the 17-grain (1.1 gram) Fragment 
Simulating Projectile (FSP). Documentation of this testing was conducted in accordance with 
the NIJ 0117.01 Public Safety Bomb Suit Standard.  
Slip Resistance - The EOD 10E foot protection has slip resistance independently proven to 
provide a static coefficient of friction of 0.60 or greater, as tested in accordance with ASTM F 
489, Standard Test Method for Static Coefficient of Friction of Shoe Sole and Heel Materials as 
measured by the James Machine, for dry conditions.  
Personal Cooling System – As documented by an independent test facility, when tested in 
accordance with ASTM F2371-05 (sweating thermal manikin testing), the EOD 10E ensemble 
provides 70 W of integrated cooling over the torso for a minimum of 120 minutes on a single 
battery serving as its primary power supply, without adding any additional weight to the suit. In 
addition, the EOD 10E ensemble is compatible with an optional body cooling system providing 
at least 145 W of average cooling to the body for a duration of at least 60 minutes. 
Warning Lights – The EOD 10E Helmet includes an LED mounted on the inside of the visor to 
notify and warn the operator of low battery level, connection problems, and other information in 
a manner not visible externally. 
Voice Command System – The EOD 10E Helmet includes an integrated Voice Command 
System that is capable of operating in multiple languages, including American English, to control 
helmet and suit functions, including ventilation, lighting (on/off, color choice, and white light 
intensity), headphone volume, and external speaker (on/off and volume).  The Voice Command 
System is interactive to facilitate usage. 
Drag Rescue System – The EOD 10E Suit jacket includes an enhanced integrated drag rescue 
system, in addition to the NIJ minimum requirements of shoulder handles, with a central 
attachment point aligned with the spine to grasp a fallen EOD operator and drag them in a 
controlled manner to a safe location for medical assistance.

Inflatable Helmet Liner – The EOD 10E Helmet includes an integrated inflatable helmet liner to 
fit the 5th percentile female to the 95th percentile male head sizes, as per the 2012 US Army 
anthropometric data. 
Helmet Lighting – The EOD 10E Helmet includes integrated LED lighting in white, red and 
blue, powered by the suit’s main power supply. Its White lights are adjustable in intensity and 
direction. The integrated lights remove the need for the operator to hold a flashlight, thereby 
freeing up the hands of the operator. Turning lights on and off, by color, is controlled by voice, to 
allow the operator to keep their hands free for other tasks.  
Control Unit Capabilities – To facilitate the work and concentration of the EOD technician, the 
EOD 10E Control Unit provides the following functional capabilities: 
• 
Display information for battery level, audio volume and fan speed  
• 
Control audio volume 
• 
Control fan speed 
• 
Countdown Timer 
• 
Stopwatch 
• 
Display fault diagnosis to assist in electronics system maintenance  
• 
Assign up to 4 pre-set user or environmental/mission profiles, such as stereophonic 
left/right speaker levels, to the electronics system to serve multiple team members who 
share a helmet 
Standard Components 
The EOD 10E ensemble includes the following components: 
• 
Jacket with integrated ventilation, back protector and detachable chest/groin plate; 
• 
Trousers with integrated yoke for trouser suspenders and an integrated waist 
cummerbund 
• 
Foot Protection 
• 
Helmet with visor, inflatable liner, integrated stereophonic speakers, microphone, and 
video camera mounting system

CITY CLERK’S OFFICE
2101 O’Neil Ave., Room 101
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001
(307) 637-6346
M E M O R A N D U M
TO:
Mayor Patrick Collins
Members of City Council
FROM:
Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk
DATE:
February 23, 2022
RE:
City Liquor License Renewals for 2022-2023
Attached is a listing of those applicants applying for renewal of existing City liquor licenses
(i.e.: retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar & grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, 
special malt beverage and winery permits), liquor licensee actions that occurred during this past
licensing year, the Police Department’s compliance report and heat maps indicating locations of all
license locations.  Public hearing for the applications is scheduled during the February 28, 2022 City
Council meeting.
Due to the large volume of paperwork involved in renewing the liquor licenses, the attached
listing provides the “basics” from application information in a readable format.  If you would like
to review an individual application file, please contact me and I will make it available to you.
If you have any questions concerning applicant information or renewal requirements and
procedures, please do not hesitate to contact me at your convenience.

Email:  cityclerk@cheyennecity.org 
 
 
 
 
December 7, 2021 
 
 
Attn:  Mr. Michael Rauzi 
Poor Richard’s 
6866 Buckskin Trail 
Cheyenne, WY   82009 
 
RE: 
Status of City of Cheyenne “parked” retail liquor license 
 
for Poor Richard’s 
 
Dear Mr. Rauzi: 
 
Pursuant to Wyoming Statute Title 12, Alcoholic Beverages, and Cheyenne City Code, Chapter 5.12, 
Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, state law provides for a two-year total time frame in which a liquor 
license may be “parked”, meaning non-operational and not open to the public.  The current license for 
Poor Richard’s was approved by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne (City Council) at its March 
8, 2021 meeting and the license has been in a “parked” inoperable status since approval of the license was 
granted. Upon showing a good cause, the governing body may extend the time period in which the 
business is required to become operational for an additional period not to exceed one year (two-years 
total). 
 
Renewal applications for the April 1, 2022 – March 31, 2023 term will be issued the end of December 
with a mid-January 2022 application deadline for submittal.  The above referenced request for an extension 
to park the license for an additional period not to exceed one year must be submitted at the time of renewal 
with required documentation to be presented to the Governing Body for review. 
 
I am enclosing a copy of Cheyenne City Code Chapter 5.12 which contains the definition of “operational” 
and restrictions on “parked” licenses.  Once the license becomes operational, it does not qualify to return 
to a “parked” status in the future unless the license is transferred in either ownership or location, which is 
contingent upon approval by the governing body. Wyoming Statutes §§12-1-101, et seq. provide 
additional information on liquor licensing and regulations. Statutes may be reviewed on-line through the 
Wyoming Legislative Service Office’s website. 
 
If you have any questions concerning what constitutes “operational” or renewal requirements, please 
contact the City Clerk’s office at your convenience. 
 
 

                  City of Cheyenne 
Municipal Building: 307.637.6200 • Main Fax: 307.637.6454 • www.cheyennecity.org • Follow us on 
Facebook & Twitter 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones 
City Clerk 
 
xc: 
City Attorney’s Office 
 
Wyoming Liquor Division 
 
File 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

January 27, 2022
Attn:
Mayor Patrick Collins
Members of The Cheyenne City Council
Wyoming Liquor Division
2101 O’Neil Avenue
Cheyenne, WY 82001
RE: Update on the status of the City of Cheyenne “parked” liquor license for Poor Richards
Dear Mr. Mayor, City Council Members and Members of the WY Liquor Division:
I am writing to request an extension to park Poor Richards liquor license for an additional
period not to exceed one year as we begin the second phase of the project at 1808 O’Neil Ave.
The first phase of our project consisted of extensive environmental testing as well as a very
involved and many months long process to get the building cleaned out.
This project has taken much longer than expected due to the condition of the 106 year old
building as well as the COVID related delays and supply chain issues that have become
common place for so many in the past year.
The liquor license will be utilized at 1808 O’Neil Ave in the same capacity as it was
previously which was a restaurant, bar and event space. We anticipate this project to be
completed and operational within 8-10 months.
We are excited to be a part of the ongoing rejuvenation of this area of downtown
Cheyenne and thank you for your patience and understanding as we continue to move forward
on this project.
Best Regards,
Michael Rauzi

Dear Mr. Mayor and City Councilors: 
We have enclosed an application for a transfer of retail liquor license number 46 that has been issued to 
Avanti, Inc. dba Dell Range Liquor.  The proposed transfer is to Dillon Companies, LLC, which is the 
owner/operator of the King Soopers store to the west of Dell Range Liquors.  In addition, the application 
seeks to expand the liquor license area from the approximately 5,600 sqft site that currently exists to an 
approximately 7,278 sqft site.    
As is required under Cheyenne’s zoning code, any expansion of a liquor license conditional use permit 
requires approval of the Cheyenne Board of Adjustment.  On December 16, 2021, the Board of 
Adjustment approved the conditional use permit expansion of the liquor store here.  A site plan shows 
the boundaries of the liquor store and liquor storage area.    
Thus, we apply for the Mayor and Council’s consideration of a transfer of ownership and approval of the 
expanded store area.  We believe that Dillon Companies, LLC will be a suitable successor to Mr. Dimarzio 
and Mr. Mancini who have operated this liquor license since 2000 as Avanti, Inc.  King Soopers has been 
a fixture in this neighborhood for many years, and we greatly enjoy being a part of the Cheyenne 
community. 
The transaction between Avanti, Inc. and Dillon Companies, LLC is contingent on the City approving the 
liquor license transfer.  Upon an approval of the transfer, the parties can quickly close.  We respectfully 
request that any approval be conditioned on the closing of the transaction between Avanti, Inc. and 
Dillon Companies, LLC. 
We look forward to speaking to you in your consideration of this proposed transfer, and appreciate the 
time you invest into consideration of items like this. 
Very truly yours, 
 
 
Teresa Dietz

Proposed Alcohol Training Program 
pursuant to City Code Chapter 5.12 requirements 
 
The Cheyenne City Code imposes the following criteria, which must be met in order for a 
licensee to meet required review criteria.  The applicant proposes to complete all of the following 
requirements, which are set forth in City Code 5.12.090.  The applicant provides “TIPS” 
(Training for Intervention ProcedureS) type training at its locations that sell alcoholic beverages, 
and so applicant is familiar with providing training and in meeting compliance requirements in 
relation to liquor sales. 
 
Applicant shall:  
 
• Provide initial orientation training on alcohol and malt beverage related sales and service 
for new employees and all volunteers who will be selling or furnishing alcohol or malt 
beverages, and on-site managers and persons who may provide on-site security services 
on behalf of the licensee or permittee upon the premises. 
 
• Provide training prior to any duties being performed that relate to alcohol and malt 
beverage sales and service. Orientation training materials must be approved by the chief 
of police. 
 
• Ensure that persons who physically sell, serve or deliver alcoholic liquor or malt 
beverages at retail on or off the premises of a licensee or permittee, and on-site managers 
and any persons providing on-site security services successfully complete an alcohol 
training program as approved by the Wyoming Liquor Division pursuant to state law and 
the division's rules and regulations. Program participants must successfully complete the 
program within ninety (90) days of employment or initial date of providing services.  
 
• Maintain, upon the business premises, a current list of bona fide employees, volunteers, 
on-site managers and any persons providing on-site security services; date of hire/service; 
dates of orientation training and successful completion of the approved training program, 
including any required continuing training. The list shall be available for inspection by 
law enforcement personnel. 
 
• Prohibit sales to and consumption by persons under the legal drinking age within the 
licensed or permitted building or authorized areas of service. 
 
• Report to local law enforcement by the licensee, permittee, employee, volunteer, on-site 
manager, agents or any persons providing on-site security services, any incidents upon 
the premises requiring law enforcement response services. Immediate notification to the 
police department shall be provided when any criminal or illegal activity occurs or is 
observed in or upon the licensee's or permittee's property involving the licensed building 
and adjoining property affiliated with the sale or consumption of alcoholic liquor and 
malt beverages, including the reporting of any assault, aggravated assault, or assault and 
battery.

My commission expires:________________ 


 
 
February 7, 2022 
Mayor and City Council 
Re: 
Application for a transfer of ownership and expansion of location as to Dell Range 
Liquors’ retail license 
Dear Mr. Mayor and City Councilors: 
We have enclosed an application for a transfer of retail liquor license number 46 that has been issued to 
Avanti, Inc. dba Dell Range Liquor.  The proposed transfer is to Dillon Companies, LLC, which is the 
owner/operator of the King Soopers store to the west of Dell Range Liquors.  In addition, the application 
seeks to expand the liquor license area from the approximately 5,600 sqft site that currently exists to an 
approximately 7,278 sqft site.    
As is required under Cheyenne’s zoning code, any expansion of a liquor license conditional use permit 
requires approval of the Cheyenne Board of Adjustment.  On December 16, 2021, the Board of 
Adjustment approved the conditional use permit expansion of the liquor store here.  A site plan shows 
the boundaries of the liquor store and liquor storage area.    
Thus, we apply for the Mayor and Council’s consideration of a transfer of ownership and approval of the 
expanded store area.  We believe that Dillon Companies, LLC will be a suitable successor to Mr. Dimarzio 
and Mr. Mancini who have operated this liquor license since 2000 as Avanti, Inc.  King Soopers has been 
a fixture in this neighborhood for many years, and we greatly enjoy being a part of the Cheyenne 
community. 
The transaction between Avanti, Inc. and Dillon Companies, LLC is contingent on the City approving the 
liquor license transfer.  Upon an approval of the transfer, the parties can quickly close.  We respectfully 
request that any approval be conditioned on the closing of the transaction between Avanti, Inc. and 
Dillon Companies, LLC. 
We look forward to speaking to you in your consideration of this proposed transfer, and appreciate the 
time you invest into consideration of items like this. 
Very truly yours, 
 
 
Teresa Dietz

Proposed Alcohol Training Program 
pursuant to City Code Chapter 5.12 requirements 
 
The Cheyenne City Code imposes the following criteria, which must be met in order for a 
licensee to meet required review criteria.  The applicant proposes to complete all of the following 
requirements, which are set forth in City Code 5.12.090.  The applicant provides “TIPS” 
(Training for Intervention ProcedureS) type training at its locations that sell alcoholic beverages, 
and so applicant is familiar with providing training and in meeting compliance requirements in 
relation to liquor sales. 
 
Applicant shall:  
 
• Provide initial orientation training on alcohol and malt beverage related sales and service 
for new employees and all volunteers who will be selling or furnishing alcohol or malt 
beverages, and on-site managers and persons who may provide on-site security services 
on behalf of the licensee or permittee upon the premises. 
 
• Provide training prior to any duties being performed that relate to alcohol and malt 
beverage sales and service. Orientation training materials must be approved by the chief 
of police. 
 
• Ensure that persons who physically sell, serve or deliver alcoholic liquor or malt 
beverages at retail on or off the premises of a licensee or permittee, and on-site managers 
and any persons providing on-site security services successfully complete an alcohol 
training program as approved by the Wyoming Liquor Division pursuant to state law and 
the division's rules and regulations. Program participants must successfully complete the 
program within ninety (90) days of employment or initial date of providing services.  
 
• Maintain, upon the business premises, a current list of bona fide employees, volunteers, 
on-site managers and any persons providing on-site security services; date of hire/service; 
dates of orientation training and successful completion of the approved training program, 
including any required continuing training. The list shall be available for inspection by 
law enforcement personnel. 
 
• Prohibit sales to and consumption by persons under the legal drinking age within the 
licensed or permitted building or authorized areas of service. 
 
• Report to local law enforcement by the licensee, permittee, employee, volunteer, on-site 
manager, agents or any persons providing on-site security services, any incidents upon 
the premises requiring law enforcement response services. Immediate notification to the 
police department shall be provided when any criminal or illegal activity occurs or is 
observed in or upon the licensee's or permittee's property involving the licensed building 
and adjoining property affiliated with the sale or consumption of alcoholic liquor and 
malt beverages, including the reporting of any assault, aggravated assault, or assault and 
battery.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-03-14_TotalExtraction_81636070.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
March 14, 2022 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym, 2811 House Ave., and via electronic conference meeting.  Present 
were: MAYOR – Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, 
Ken Esquibel (arriving at 7:35 via Zoom), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  Also present: Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of 
allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Dr. Aldrich, agenda item #20 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Johnson, 
agenda item #28(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request by Mr. Segrave, and 
pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Johnson, agenda item #28(d) was removed 
from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 28, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on March 3, 2022.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
         
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) of the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to Table 5-1: Zoning District Uses in UDC Section 5.1.4, Districts 
and Uses, amending the Permissibility of Various Uses.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, 
Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#4417) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Common Landscaping Plan and definition of Development Footprint.  Mr. Cook moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Cook moved to postpone to 
March 28, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Charles Bloom, Director of Planning and 
Development, advised discussions had occurred with individuals that had concerns and they would 
like to prepare amendments to address those concerns.  Mr. Bloom noted there will likely be an 
additional postponement requested.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. 
 

ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. (#4418) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use to AG Agricultural for 
land located east of Southwest Drive, south of I-80.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on third and final 
reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present. (#4419) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to HI Heavy 
Industrial for land located north of Campstool Road, west of Progress Circle.  Mr. Cook moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#4420) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 9.08.120, Malicious Harassment 
Based on Race, Color, Religion, Ancestry, Sex, Sexual Orientation, Gender Identity and 
Expression, National Origin, or Disability, or Political Affiliation of Chapter 9.08, Offenses 
Against Public Peace and Decency, of Title 9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mayor Collins announced there would be a 3-minute time limit for 
speakers.  Speaking in opposition of the ordinance were: Bob Wilson, Tom Perea, Steve Milliad, 
John Romero Martinez and Lynn Hutchings.  Topics discussed included: existing statutes, 
ordinances and policies that address bullying, harassment and violence; potential of ordinance 
being harmful towards individuals with opposing beliefs and being used as a wedge; confusion 
and challenges relating to the ordinance; biblical beliefs; how reporting offences to the Police 
Department will be handled; belief that Cheyenne is not in need of the ordinance; the need for the 
community to come together to address malicious harassment issues without an ordinance and 
civil rights.  Speaking in support of the ordinance were: Amber Leyba and Dale Steenbergen, 
Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, James Peebles, Dr. Larry Meuli, and Sarah Burlingame, 
Director of Wyoming Equality.  Topics discussed included: Governing Body support for the 
ordinance; ordinance importance relating to economic development; anti-lynching senate bill; 
issues in Ukraine; importance of ordinance to protect children and military lives; existing 
harassment and discrimination in the community; city’s poor economic development ranking, and 
the ordinance contributing to everyone feeling safe and supported.  Additional comments were 
made related to supporting the spirit of the ordinance but opposition to the way the ordinance is 
written.  Dr. Aldrich read a statement related to a newspaper editorial and apologized for 
misspeaking.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. (#4421) 
 
 
 
 

ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 12.20, Cemeteries, 
of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne pertaining 
to Cemetery fees.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. White moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4422) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) 
repealing UDC 5.4.7, UU – Urban Use Overlay District, and creating UDC 5.6.9, UU – Urban Use 
Overlay District.  Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from P Public District to CB Community Business 
for land located southwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Cleveland Avenue and U.S. 30.  
Mr. Cook moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried. Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis to CB 
Community Business for land located southeast of the intersection of College Drive and Charles 
Street – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Creating a new Section 9.01.010, Definitions of Title 9, 
Public Peace and Welfare, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public 
Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Johnson) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Adopting a fee schedule for Planning and Development projects. 
(Postponed from February 28, 2022).  Mr. White moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Mr. 
White moved to amend by substitute dated March 4, 2022, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Charles 
Bloom, Director of Planning and Development, provided information on the proposed substitute 
which includes: removal of the appraisal cost fee from the street/alley vacation by ordinance 
application, $2,500 limit for preliminary and final plat applications, $5,200 limit for expedited plat 
applications which is the sum of the preliminary and final plat applications plus a $200 fee and 
requirement for staff review of the fee schedule every 5 years. Mr. Bloom noted he has received 
supportive feedback related to the proposed amendment.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bloom advised the 
last time the fee schedule was reviewed was October 25, 1993.  Appreciation was expressed to Mr. 
Bloom and his staff for review of the fee schedule.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes”– all members of the governing body present.  Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#6213)

RESOLUTION – Permitting the possession of open containers within the Downtown 
District Boundaries for the ‘Tales and Ales’ Pub Crawl to be held on April 2, 2022.  Domenic 
Bravo, President and CEO of Visit Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Downtown Development 
Authority, provided information on the event which involves open containers from establishments 
to trolleys which requires the resolution.  Mr. Cook moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Concern was expressed related to the annual open container period including the DDA boundaries 
and how the open container period is managed.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Bravo advised all 4 trolleys 
will be utilized for the event, approximately 50 tickets have been sold to date and the event will be 
held from 3-6 p.m. on April 2, 2022.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing 
body present. (#6215)

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team 
(CRAFT) for Minimum Revenue Guarantee Payment (General Fund).  Wendy Volk, President of 
the Cheyenne Regional Air Service Focus Team (CRAFT), provided information on the agreement 
noting the agreement should have been signed last summer, therefore, it is retroactive.  She 
explained there was a delay with the runway project which ended up taking 4 months instead of 
11 weeks, and there was no air service during this period.  Ms. Volk advised 8,500 passengers 
have used air service since the runway project has been complete.  Ms. Volk provided further 
information on current scheduled flights and future proposed flights.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Volk 
explained the agreement will run through June 30, 2022 and 6th Penny funds will start being 
collected June 1st.  Mr. White moved to approve in an amount not to exceed $240,000.00, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
(#7483) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Fire and Rescue 
Department, and UC Health c/o Cheyenne Medical Specialist to provide occupational medical 
exams (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$50,000.00). (#7484) 
 
 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Carbon Asset 
Network (CAN) establishing terms and conditions for land manager partnerships.  Dale 
Steenbergen, President and CEO of the Greater Cheyenne Chamber of Commerce, expressed 
continued support for the MOU, noting it is a great opportunity for community.  Mr. White moved 
to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated March 9, 
2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne explained the substitute removes conflicting 
termination language, corrects typographical errors and adds greater liability to CAN specifically 
if the city is acting on information provided by CAN.  Upon inquiry, John Robitaille, CAN, advised 
at this time it is hard to predict income the city may receive as they are working on creating a new 
commodity and it depends on how much someone may be willing to pay for a ton of carbon stored 
in soil, but they are hoping to get $30/month per acre.  The city would then receive 2% of the fees 
collected. Mr. Robitaille noted currently there are 250,000 acres in network and they will continue 
marketing and increasing acreage over time. Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised it has not been 
determined which city department will administer the land manager partnerships. Comments were 
made in support of the concept and substitute and concern was expressed for how much 
involvement may be required by the city with only receiving 2% of collected fees.  Motion to 
amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Further 
comments were made in support of the MOU with hopes the process will continue to grow, the 
importance of the city’s participation and preference not to duplicate efforts that have already been 
done in areas such as the Belvoir Ranch with water and soil testing.  Main motion as amended by 
substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception 
of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#7485) 
 

 
Agreement between the Board of Public Utilities and Polo Ranch Company regarding 
Quarter Circle Five subdivision well development and associated water entitlement.  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Comment was made that the proposed agreement 
doesn’t contain any language that would prevent the developer from suing the city and there have 
been a number of lawsuits regarding the development and who is in control of the property.  Upon 
inquiry, Mayor Collins turned over duties of the Chair to City Council president Mr. Roybal and 
provided information on the Polo Ranch.  Mayor Collins explained in 1955 the city bought the 
water rights and the ranch has been purchased by another Party and it has been attempted to rescind 
the ownership.  The property was subdivided several years ago, and several homes were given 
water illegally. Concern was expressed relating to the possibility of another lawsuit. Mr. Collins 
moved to postpone to March 28, 2022, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

Renewal applications for existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, 
resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, special malt beverage and winery 
permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  Piper Rauzi, Poor Richard’s, provided an update on 
their business plan and the building at 1808 O’Neil Ave. where their retail liquor license is parked. 
Ms. Rauzi explained environmental testing and building cleanout has occurred at the historic 
property.  The buildout will begin later this month which will include Phase I which will be a bar. 
She provided further information on the building explaining new garage doors have been installed 
through a DDA grant and it took a long time to get the doors in.  As the first allowed year period 
for parking the license is coming to an end, they would like to request to park the license for 
another year but expect the business to open in the next 4-5 months.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Rauzi 
explained the business remodel will involve 2 phases – Phase I will be opening the front of the 
building as a bar and Phase II will be involving the back part of the building to include a concert 
venue.  She noted food will also be served in some capacity.  Comments were made related to 
concern of a retail liquor license being in a parked status for 2 years and not collecting any sales 
tax dollars when there are so many establishments seeking that type of license.  Mr. White moved 
to approve, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. White moved to divide the question to consider separate 
action on the application for renewal of the retail liquor license for Poor Richard’s, seconded by 
Dr. Aldrich.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. White.  
Voting “no” - Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave.  Mr. White 
moved to divide the question to consider separate action on the establishments which have not 
provided TIPS lists during routine compliance checks.  These establishments include: Eagle’s 
Nest; Big D Oil #29; Vino’s Wine & Spirits; Accomplice Beer Co.; The Plains Hotel; Redwood 
Lounge and Package Liquors; China Buffet; Four Winds, Inc. and Peppermill Bar & Liquor, 

seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” - Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Laybourn.  Voting 
“no” – Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr.  Segrave 
and Mr. White.  Comment was made relating to non-compliance of certain establishment 
concerning TIPS training all of which have become compliant since the Finance Committee 
meeting. The request was expressed that next year all establishments be able to provide TIPS 
training information when it is first requested during Police Department compliance checks.  
Further comment was made relating to how liquor licenses have been issued over the years and 
that some establishments initially issued retail licenses may be able to utilize bar and grill or 
restaurant liquor licenses and how it would be economically beneficial to be able to review these 
scenarios.  Main Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. [Clerk’s 
note: Mr. Esquibel joined the meeting via Zoom at 7:35 p.m. during discussion of this item] 
 
Transfer of ownership and location of retail liquor license application filed with the City 
Clerk’s Office for Dillon Companies, LLC, a Kansas limited liability company d/b/a King Soopers, 
3702 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by Avanti, Inc., d/b/a Dell 
Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. #4, Cheyenne, Wyoming).  Mr. White advised there was no 
recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Teresa Dietz, Adult Beverage Specialist, 
King Soopers, explained that along with extending their existing space for the package liquor store, 
they will be moving the grocery pick up location and will be able to increase pick up orders for 
the community.  She noted all liquor store sales will take place in the designated space which will 
also include some groceries.  Justin Flohr, Store Manager of Cheyenne King Soopers, expressed 
appreciation for consideration of the license transfer and noted the transfer will allow for the 
possibility of career advancements for store associates.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded 
by Mr. Johnson.  Comments were made on the following topics: concern with alcohol sales in 
grocery stores; high demand for retail liquor licenses; the perception that the King Soopers 
business isn’t in dire need of the license; other similar businesses that have received retail liquor 
licenses locally; lack of available liquor licenses at city and state levels; economic need for 
different business types such as a restaurant or unique bar to hold a retail liquor license; history of 
license as originally issued to Avanti’s for a small specialty wines package store and some citizen 
support for being able to purchase alcohol at a grocery store.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Stefanie 
Boster upon inquiry advised the governing body isn’t bound by precedence in their decision to 
approve or deny the transfer.  Ms. Boster referred to case law and state statutes, noting certain 
guidelines must be followed and evidence should be considered when they are reviewing the 
transfer.  Further comments were made regarding liquor sales in grocery/convenience stores and 
economic needs and growth.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Boster explained that since the renewal was 
approved for Dell Range Liquor, if the transfer isn’t approved, the license will remain with Dell 
Range Liquor.  It was further noted that there likely won’t be many businesses that will be able to 
purchase a license at market rate.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. 
Laybourn and Mr. Segrave.  Voting “no” – Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Esquibel, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal and 
Mr. White.  City Clerk Kris Jones noted for the record that she received a text at 7:35 p.m. that 
Mr. Esquibel had joined the meeting via Zoom during liquor license renewals discussion.  Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Boster explained pursuant to Roberts Rule of Order, a member can arrive late, and 
she believes Mr. Esquibel’s vote is appropriate. 
             
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 

 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 Central Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (license currently held by Dillinger’s, LLC d/b/a Dillinger’s, 1601 Central Ave., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at 
Storey Gym (LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic 
conference meeting, for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for Wyoming Horse Racing LLC d/b/a Horse Palace, 1601 Central Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (license held by Prime Realty LLC d/b/a Prime Realty, 1601 Central Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming). 
 
 
Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held March 28, 2022 at 6:00 p.m. at Storey Gym 
(LCSD #1 Board Room), 2811 House Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Osterfoss 
Living Trust Dated May 20, 2020 d/b/a Nagel-Warren Mansion, LLC, 222 E. 17th St., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming. 
 
 
Under other business:   
 
 
Jim Ridgeway expressed concern relating to the city’s surplus sale of Cheyenne Police 
Department evidence seized firearms requirements.  TJ Barttlebort, Purchasing Manager, 
explained he had spoken to Mr. Ridgeway and provided information on the surplus sale noting 2 
bids are currently being solicited for evidence seized firearms.  He advised it is the Police 
Department’s request to award the bid to someone residing more than 100 miles away from 
Cheyenne and also to one person submitting the highest responsive bid for all firearms.  Mayor 
Collins noted the bid will come before the Governing Body for approval.  Mr. Barttlebort noted 
he will have further discussions with the Police Department and that there is time to amend process 
if needed.  
 
 
Judi Just commented on the city’s consideration of going to a self-insured insurance plan 
and explained a situation previously when the city was self-insured and suffered great financial 
loss due to the plan. She provided information on recent legislative bills that if passed, could have 
negatively affected self-insured plans.  
 
 
Mr. White noted the motion to adopt the following Resolution - Authorizing the Mayor 
and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Sweetgrass, 9th Filing, a Replat of all of Lot 1, Block 4, 
Sweetgrass, 1st Filing situated in the N1/2 of Section 16, Township 13 North, Range 66 West of 
the 6th P.M., City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located south of East Murray Drive 
west and adjacent to Sweetgrass Drive), failed at the February 28, 2022 City Council meeting.  
Having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to Chapter 2.04 of City Code, Mr. White moved 
to reconsider the vote taken on the resolution, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Casey Palma, Palma Land 
Planning, agent for the owner, advised information on the development was distributed to members 
of the governing body.  Mr. White explained that following subsequent meetings with the agent 

and developers of the project, his concerns regarding parking and density of the project have been 
alleviated and requested support for the motion to reconsider.  Mr. White further explained that he 
is no longer concerned about parking and believes it will be sufficient.  Comment was made that 
there has been no additional information since the final vote was taken, the area remains limited, 
the original plan had an acre of open space and that this plan has very little open space.   Mr. 
Palma, upon inquiry, advised that code would require 174 parking spaces and 68 bike parking 
spaces and that the plan has been increased from what code would require.  He noted the units will 
be available at current market rate.  Comments were made that following a tour of the property, 
the project is something that can be compared to other developments of this nature in the city and 
to take into consideration the entire project, open space and park land involved and that 
development projects are essentially to make money for the developer.  Motion to reconsider 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave 
voting “no”.  Dr.  Rinne moved to postpone for 2 weeks, seconded by Laybourn.  Dr. Rinne 
explained that historically when considering a reconsideration of an agenda item, it has been 
customary to postpone the item to allow for interested parties to become aware and have an 
opportunity to participate in the discussion.  It was noted that this item has had 5 public hearings 
and those who have wanted to comment have had the opportunity to do so.  Motion to postpone 
failed.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White.  Voting 
“yes” – Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal.    Upon inquiry 
from Mayor Collins, City Clerk Kris Jones read the Resolution entitlement into the record.  Mr. 
Palma provided clarification from a previous discussion on open space and noted there is a private 
park with amenities.  Upon inquiry, James Powers, representative of the applicant and realtor with 
Caldwell Banker, advised the developer is local and expressed appreciation for reconsidering the 
item.  Mr. Powers further noted that Summit Management sets a precedent for developers, the city 
has put together an affordable task force for 3,000 housing units for short term rentals, rents are 
climbing and will continue to do so and that this project will benefit the community.  He advised 
the proposal is a reduction of 30% in density and that he has worked with city staff to bring a 
project forward that meets approval.  It was noted the developer is not the original family, that the 
governing body is not opposed to growth, but they do want quality growth.  Concern was raised 
on adequate parking for the project.  Mr. Powers provided examples of previous developments 
such as Carter Cottages with a less acceptable parking ratio.  He advised that the property owner 
is participating in and supports the project.  Chris Lovas, Lovas Engineering, advised the applicant 
has invested in the engineering plans which allows them to work with BOPU and city engineering 
staff to ensure the project’s success from an engineering standpoint.  Additional comment was 
made that market rate for the proposed development units may not be affordable and caution was 
raised regarding the housing shortage and affordable housing.  Mr. Palma, upon inquiry, confirmed 
that proposed parking and open space meets current UDC requirements.  It was noted that if the 
development wasn’t in a PUD, it would have been approved administratively.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no  
 
 
Dr. Aldrich advised there will be a City Council Ward III Council and Coffee event on 
Saturday, March 19th, from 9-11 a.m. at Dazbog Coffee (3911 E. Pershing Blvd.) 
 
 
Mr. Esquibel apologized for failing to notify members of the governing body of his late 
arrival.  
 

 
Mayor Collins requested the Governing Body meet in executive session to discuss 
litigation matters and a proposed facility purchase.  Mr. Roybal moved to meet in executive session 
pursuant to Wyoming Statute §16-4-405(a)(iii)(vii)(x), seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Mayor Collins announced members 
would meet in executive session, not open to the public; requested the City Attorney, City Clerk, 
City Treasurer and Chief of Staff be in attendance and stated there would be no action taken by 
the governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting 
would officially conclude at the close of the executive session. Mayor Collins further advised that 
the following staff members would be present in executive session respective to topics relating to 
their departments/divisions:  Compliance Director, Public Works Director, Transit Director, Fire 
Chief and the Fire Marshal. (Clerk’s Note: executive session convened at 8:47 p.m.).  
  
 
Following closure of the executive session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular meeting 
at 9:48 p.m. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 9:48 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones,   
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 23, 2022
